4200 B8CE C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
4201 B8D0 0450  20 	b *r0
4202               	;
4203               	; 	' ======================================================================
4204               	; 	' DRAWING THE STORE -- four blits, once per screen crossing
4205               	; 	' ======================================================================
4206               	; 	' This is the whole cost of the flip, and it is paid on a discrete event
4207               	; 	' rather than every frame. RallyX's pan repaints 576 chars every time the
4208               	; 	' camera moves one cell; this repaints 640 about once every two seconds
4209               	; 	' of running.
4210               	; 	' SPRITES OFF BEFORE THE BLIT, NOT AFTER IT. This routine WAITs between
4211               	; 	' bands -- one band a frame, because a burst of VDP writes past a few
4212               	; 	' dozen in one frame is silently dropped -- so painting the new screen
4213               	; 	' takes several frames, and for all of them the OLD screen's actors and
4214               	; 	' obstacles were still sitting on top of it. Following Harry through a
4215               	; 	' seam showed him and every cart from the floor he had just left,
4216               	; 	' standing on a shop that was assembling itself underneath them.
4217               	; 	'
4218               	; 	' They are put back by the normal draw on the same pass, so the crossing
4219               	; 	' reads as a cut rather than as a dissolve with ghosts in it.
4220               	; 	' EACH BAND IS FINISHED BEFORE THE FRAME IT IS DRAWN IN ENDS, and the
4221               	; 	' bands go TOP DOWN. Both halves of that matter.
4222               	; 	'
4223               	; 	' The blit paints the template -- shelves, pillars and all -- and the
4224               	; 	' radios and prizes then take out the fixtures they stand in. Those two
4225               	; 	' used to be four frames apart: all four bands blitted with a WAIT
4226               	; 	' between them, and only afterwards the clearing. So every screen change
4227               	; 	' showed its counters and beams for several frames and then erased them,
4228               	; 	' which reads as the shop flickering rather than as it being drawn.
4229               	; 	'
4230               	; 	' TOP DOWN because a band's beam tops are stamped into the SLAB ROW OF
4231               	; 	' THE BAND ABOVE. Going bottom-up, band 1's blit would paint over the
4232               	; 	' beam tops band 0 had just put there. Going top-down the band above is
4233               	; 	' always already drawn, so everything a band needs -- its own cells and
4234               	; 	' the row it borrows overhead -- is final by the time its WAIT arrives.
4235               	; 	'
4236               	; 	' `3 - dq` rather than `FOR dlv = 3 TO 0 STEP -1`: dlv is an unsigned
4237               	; 	' byte, so counting down past zero wraps to 255 and the loop never ends
4238               	; 	' (CLAUDE.md 3A).
4239               	; draw_screen:
4240               cvb_DRAW_SCREEN
4241               	; 	GOSUB hide_play
4242 B8D2 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     B8D4 A980     
4243 B8D6 E858     	data cvb_HIDE_PLAY
4244               	; 	GOSUB load_band
4245 B8D8 06A0  32 	bl @jsr
     B8DA A980     
4246 B8DC BCC6     	data cvb_LOAD_BAND

4341               	;
4342               	; 	' A SUPPORT BEAM HAS TO REACH THE FLOOR IT HOLDS UP, AND THAT FLOOR IS
4343               	; 	' NOT IN ITS OWN BAND. A beam fills its band's four air rows, so its top
4344               	; 	' pixel sits under the slab row of the band ABOVE -- and a slab row is
4345               	; 	' five pixels of bar over three of green (see SLAB), so the beam stopped
4346               	; 	' three pixels short of the bar on every storey. It read as a beam that
4347               	; 	' misses the floor, which is exactly what it was.
4348               	; 	'
4349               	; 	' It cannot be drawn into either template. The beam belongs to the band
4350               	; 	' below and the row belongs to the band above, and the two templates are
4351               	; 	' chosen independently per screen, so no template knows both. So the
4352               	; 	' band blits go down first and the tops are stamped afterwards, from a
4353               	; 	' per-template column table -- the same shape as the escalator's head cap.
4354               	; 	'
4355               	; 	' Bands 0 and 1 only. Band 2's top row is the ROOF DECK, which is grey
4356               	; 	' over grey with no green to bridge, and the roof itself has no band
4357               	; 	' above it at all.
4358               	; 	' BANDS 0 TO 2 -- ALL THREE SHOPPING FLOORS. It used to stop at 1,
4359               	; 	' because band 2's ceiling is the ROOF DECK and the deck had no green
4360               	; 	' under it: a beam there already ran straight into grey and needed
4361               	; 	' nothing. Giving the roof its three green rows (so the lift shaft stops
4362               	; 	' under it, like every other floor) took that away, and the top floor's
4363               	; 	' beams started falling three pixels short.
4364               	; 	'
4365               	; 	' So the roof gets its own version of the stamp. A beam HOLDS THE ROOF
4366               	; 	' UP and must reach it; the shaft merely stops beneath it.
4367               	; beam_one:
4368               cvb_BEAM_ONE
4369               	; 	IF bt > 2 THEN RETURN		' the roof has no band above it

4902               	; 			' NO SPRITE HAZARD ON THE FLOOR THE CROOK IS STANDING ON,
4903               	; 			' AND THE DECISION IS MADE HERE -- once, as the screen is
4904               	; 			' drawn, and never revisited while it is on screen.
4905               	; 			'
4906               	; 			' It was a test in the draw loop and another in the
4907               	; 			' collision test, both re-evaluated every frame, so the
4908               	; 			' hazards came and went as Harry walked on and off the
4909               	; 			' screen: run him to the edge and a floor's worth of balls
4910               	; 			' appeared out of nothing behind him. A rule about what
4911               	; 			' this screen CONTAINS cannot be a per-frame question --
4912               	; 			' the answer changes while the player is looking at it.
4913               	; 			'
4914               	; 			' Zeroing the kind here also makes both of those tests
4915               	; 			' unnecessary: an obstacle that was never loaded cannot be
4916               	; 			' drawn and cannot hit anybody.
4917               	; 			'
4918               	; 			' Radios are exempt. They are characters rather than
4919               	; 			' sprites, they do not move, and a fixture the crook runs
4920               	; 			' past is not what this rule is about.

4998               	; 			' THEY COME FROM THE FAR SIDE. Kelly walked into this screen
4999               	; 			' from one edge, so the obstacles start at the OTHER edge and
5000               	; 			' run toward him. Crossing a seam then always presents an
5001               	; 			' ONCOMING stream, never a set of backs he has to catch up
5002               	; 			' with -- and it means the direction he is travelling is
5003               	; 			' always the direction the danger comes from.

5006               	; 			' an absolute position, and the slot index spreads the three

     BF5E 225A     
5082 BF60 D401  30 	movb r1,*r0
5083               	; 			' THE BALL IS ON THE GROUND WHEN HE GETS TO IT.
5084               	; 			'
5085               	; 			' The bounce phase used to be `lx AND 31` -- a byte out of
5086               	; 			' the placement table, which is to say arbitrary. Run onto
5087               	; 			' a screen and the first ball might be at the bottom of its
5088               	; 			' arc, where a jump clears it, or at the top, where it has
5089               	; 			' to be ducked, and nothing on the way in told you which.
5090               	; 			' Sometimes you could keep running and jump it; sometimes
5091               	; 			' the same approach at the same speed could not be jumped
5092               	; 			' at all. That is not difficulty, it is a coin toss.
5093               	; 			'
5094               	; 			' So the phase is worked BACKWARDS from the meeting. He
5095               	; 			' closes 4 px a pass and the ball 2, so they meet in
5096               	; 			' (gap / 6) passes; the phase advances one a pass and wraps
5097               	; 			' at 32, so seeding it with the NEGATIVE of that lands it on
5098               	; 			' phase 0 -- the ground -- exactly as they arrive.
5099               	; 			'
5100               	; 			' The gap is the same either way round: he enters at one
5101               	; 			' wall and the ball starts `stag` in from the other, so it
5102               	; 			' is 232 - stag whichever direction the floor runs.
5103               	; 			'
5104               	; 			' It stays forgiving rather than exact. The arc is flat near
5105               	; 			' the bottom, so anything within eight passes either side is
5106               	; 			' still under the jump; and it only holds for an unbroken
5107               	; 			' run, which is the case the player is entitled to read off
5108               	; 			' the screen. Duck, stop or take a hit and everything after
5109               	; 			' is out of step again -- which is the game.
5110               	; 			obp(li) = lx AND 31
5111 BF62 D020  34 	movb @cvb_LX,r0
     BF64 21C8     
5112 BF66 0201  20 	li r1,7936   ; 31*256
     BF68 1F00     
5113 BF6A 0541  14 	inv r1
5114 BF6C 5001  18 	szcb r1,r0
5115 BF6E C040  18 	mov r0,r1

     BF72 21B8

     C666 C704     
5923               cv292
5924               	; 		' HE IS STANDING ON A STEP, so he moves at the STEP's rate: 2 px
5925               	; 		' across and 1 up PER PASS -- the staircase's own slope, and exactly
5926               	; 		' what one phase of esc_tick moves the pattern by.
5927               	; 		'
5928               	; 		' NOT `fdv`, WHICH IS THE WHOLE POINT. Everything else in this loop
5929               	; 		' is paced by the frame delta, and pacing him that way too made him
5930               	; 		' outrun the steps the moment a pass cost more than one frame: his
5931               	; 		' feet started planted on a tread and ended floating above it.
5932               	; 		' Pacing the ANIMATION by fdv instead is worse -- four phases cannot
5933               	; 		' be stepped by 2 or 3 without aliasing, and the staircase runs
5934               	; 		' backwards (see esc_tick). The only arrangement that cannot drift
5935               	; 		' is the one where both are driven by the same fixed step, so the
5936               	; 		' rider is clocked by the pass, like the steps he is standing on.
5937               	; 		'
5938               	; 		' THE FIRST FEW PASSES ARE THE STEP ON. Walking on, his feet are on
5939               	; 		' the floor and the bottom tread is 4 to 7 px above them, so he
5940               	; 		' climbs at 2 px a pass until he catches it (the tread is rising at
5941               	; 		' 1, so the gap closes at 1 a pass) and then rides with it. Jumping
5942               	; 		' on he lands ON a tread, so eson is 0 and this never runs.
5943               	; 		IF esy < eson THEN esy = esy + 1
5944 C668 D020  34 	movb @cvb_ESY,r0

6573               	;
6574               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------------------ escalator
6575               	; 	' ONE working escalator per floor, at that floor's alternating end --
6576               	; 	' west, east, west. An escalator drawn where none works would be a step
6577               	; 	' the player runs to and nothing happens.
6578               	; 	' ESCALATORS RUN BOTH WAYS. Stand at the foot and you are carried up;
6579               	; 	' stand at the head of the flight that arrives from the floor below and
6580               	; 	' you are carried down. It is one staircase used from either end, not two
6581               	; 	' -- which is also why the down zone on a floor is the UP zone of the
6582               	; 	' floor beneath it, offset by one band.
6583               	; 	' UP ONLY. Riding down was built at one point and has been taken out
6584               	; 	' again at the reviewer's direction -- these only ever climb.
6585               	; 	'
6586               	; 	' Losing it also removed the re-board lock, which existed solely because
6587               	; 	' stepping off at the head put you inside the zone that rode the same
6588               	; 	' flight back down. With no down ride there is no such zone: a floor's
6589               	; 	' escalator is at the opposite end from the one below it, so arriving at
6590               	; 	' the top of one never lands you on the foot of another.

7149               	;
7150               	; 	' THE VICTROLA, AS CHARACTERS. It never moves, so a sprite spent a
7151               	; 	' scan-line slot on something that can be painted once when the screen is
7152               	; 	' drawn -- and at one 16x16 sprite it could only ever be one colour and
7153               	; 	' one size. Four cells is twice the area for no sprite at all.
7154               	; 	'
7155               	; 	' It stands ON the slab, so it fills the band's rows 2 and 3; row 4 is
7156               	; 	' the floor bar itself and draw_prizes uses row 3 for a collectible,
7157               	; 	' which is why the two never share a screen (genstore keeps prizes off
7158               	; 	' the screens that carry hazards).
7159               	; 	'
7160               	; 	' rdall = 1 paints all four cells, 0 repaints only the top-right one --
7161               	; 	' the pulse. Same address arithmetic either way, so the two cannot drift.
7162               	; 	' A BEAM TOP HANGS OUT OF THE FLOOR ABOVE, and the beam it belongs to has
7163               	; 	' just been taken away. `beam_tops` stamps SLABP -- the floor bar with the
7164               	; 	' support carried up through its lower three pixel rows -- into the slab
7165               	; 	' row above every pillar, and it runs BEFORE the radios and prizes are
7166               	; 	' placed. Clearing a pillar's own cells therefore left three grey pixels
7167               	; 	' dangling from the ceiling with nothing under them.
7168               	; 	'
7169               	; 	' TESTED, NOT ASSUMED: only SLABP is put back to SLAB. The row above the
7170               	; 	' top shopping floor is the ROOF DECK, a different character altogether,
7171               	; 	' and writing a floor bar over it would punch a hole in the roof.
7172               	; 	' A SHELF RUN IS FIVE CELLS WIDE and a radio covers two, so one standing
7173               	; 	' on a counter punched a hole through its middle and left the ends either
7174               	; 	' side -- the same incoherence the dangling pillar had, and worse, because
7175               	; 	' a counter reads as one object. The whole unit goes: walk outward along
7176               	; 	' the band's row 2 while the cell is still a shelf top, clearing it and
7177               	; 	' its bottom half together.
7178               	; 	'
7179               	; 	' Bounded at eight steps. The runs are five, so eight is slack rather
7180               	; 	' than a guess -- and an unbounded walk would run off the end of the row
7181               	; 	' if the name table ever held something unexpected.
7182               	; wipe_shelf:
7183               cvb_WIPE_SHELF
7184               	; 	wsn = 8

7606               	;
7607               	; 	' THE SOUND PULSE. Driven off fphs, which already ticks once per pass and
7608               	; 	' is immune to the frame delta, and repainted only when the phase BIT
7609               	; 	' actually flips -- otherwise this would be eight VDP writes every pass
7610               	; 	' for a picture that had not changed (CLAUDE.md 3A: bursts past a few
7611               	; 	' dozen a frame are silently dropped).
7612               	; 	' EVERY BAND'S RADIOS -- what the pulse needs, since it repaints the top
7613               	; 	' cells wherever they are.
7614               	; radio_draw:
7615               cvb_RADIO_DRAW

     D318 2176

8268               	;
8269               	; 	' THE QUARTER-PIXEL ACCUMULATOR. hx is one unsigned byte per screen (2),
8270               	; 	' so there is nowhere to keep a fraction -- the fraction lives here and
8271               	; 	' is spent as whole pixels. hsp4 = 6 walks 1,2,1,2 (1.5 px/frame); 7
8272               	; 	' walks 1,2,2,2 over four frames (1.75); 8 is a flat 2. Two IFs, not a
8273               	; 	' loop and not a divide: hacc is under 4 on entry and hsp4 is at most 8,
8274               	; 	' so it can never need a third. `%` and `/` both compile to a real
8275               	; 	' TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and this runs every frame.
8276               	; 	'
8277               	; 	' 7, NOT 6, AND THE REASON IS MEASURED. Tracked off the reference video
8278               	; 	' (DESIGN.md 4a), the 2600's Kelly covers 0.40 screen widths a second and
8279               	; 	' its Harry 0.193 -- a ratio of 2.07. At hsp4 = 6 ours was 2.67, and the
8280               	; 	' crook visibly strolled. 7 brings it to 2.29. 8 would match the original
8281               	; 	' exactly and cannot be used: Kelly's route here is 1.95x Harry's, so a
8282               	; 	' speed ratio of 2.0 leaves the on-foot chase 0.2 s of slack, which is no
8283               	; 	' chase at all. checkchase.py holds the line.
8284               	; 	' STILL RUNNING FROM THE STAIRS -- and this has to come BEFORE the move,
8285               	; 	' not after it. It sat below the two `IF hmv` blocks, where all it could
8286               	; 	' still reach was the animation counter: he faced the right way, played
8287               	; 	' the run cycle and did not travel a pixel. He stood at the foot of the
8288               	; 	' flight looking busy.
8289               	; 	'
8290               	; 	' TWO THINGS END THE RUN, AND BOTH ARE VISIBLE ON SCREEN.
8291               	; 	'
8292               	; 	' THE KOP ARRIVING ON THIS FLOOR OUTRANKS IT. This used to override the
8293               	; 	' flee as well as the climb, so for the whole of the run he held one
8294               	; 	' heading no matter where Kelly was -- and ran straight into him without
8295               	; 	' appearing to notice he was there. The flee above has already worked out
8296               	; 	' which way is away; clearing the run simply lets it stand.
8297               	; 	'
8298               	; 	' AND REACHING THE END WALL ENDS IT. It used to be a count of passes,

8834               	;
8835               	; 	' ======================================================================
8836               	; 	' COLLISIONS -- pure arithmetic against RAM, no VDP reads
8837               	; 	' ======================================================================
8838               	; 	' Kelly's box is [kfh, kfh + kh) above the slab; an obstacle's is
8839               	; 	' [ohb, oht). The ball's inset hitbox is what makes 8..10 avoidable both
8840               	; 	' ways -- without it there is a height at which the ball can be neither
8841               	; 	' jumped nor ducked, and an unavoidable hazard is not difficulty.
8842               	; 	' ONE HIT PER CONTACT, WITH A REFRACTORY PERIOD.
8843               	; 	'
8844               	; 	' The overlap test is true on every frame the player is touching
8845               	; 	' something, so with no latch at all one clumsy cart charges nine seconds
8846               	; 	' several times over -- and what the player sees is the clock jumping by
8847               	; 	' 27, from which the only available conclusion is that the penalty is
8848               	; 	' broken.
8849               	; 	'
8850               	; 	' A latch that clears on the first non-overlapping frame is still not
8851               	; 	' enough, and the case that breaks it is the BOUNCING BALL: it rises off
8852               	; 	' the player between bounces, which is a genuine loss of contact, so a
8853               	; 	' player standing still under one is taxed on every single bounce while
8854               	; 	' being given no opportunity to do anything about it. Technically two
8855               	; 	' collisions; in play, one situation.
8856               	; 	'
8857               	; 	' So obht() is a countdown, not a flag. A hit sets it to HITREF; it ticks
8858               	; 	' down only while the player is CLEAR, and the same obstacle cannot charge
8859               	; 	' again until it reaches zero. Contact holds the count up, so parking
8860               	; 	' inside something never earns a second penalty either.
8861               	; coll_obst:
8862               cvb_COLL_OBST
8863               	; 	IF klst = ST_ESC THEN RETURN		' riding is invincible, per the
8864 DC62 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLST,r0
     DC64 218C     
8865 DC66 0201  20 	li r1,768   ; 3*256
     DC68 0300     
8866 DC6A 9001  18 	cb r1,r0
8867 DC6C 1302  14 	jeq cv652
8868 DC6E 0460  28 	b @cv651
     DC70 DC76

9129               	;

9398               	; 	' NO +8 ON EITHER SIDE, AND THAT IS THE FIX RATHER THAN A TIDY-UP. Both

9400               	; 	' difference -- but adding it first OVERFLOWED THE BYTE. Harry walks to
9401               	; 	' x 253 before he crosses a seam, and 253 + 8 wraps to 5, so a Kop
9402               	; 	' standing at the far LEFT of the screen measured five pixels to a crook
9403               	; 	' walking off the far RIGHT and arrested him across the whole store.
9404               	; 	'
9405               	; 	' Nothing warned: both are plain 8-bit variables, the wrap is silent, and
9406               	; 	' the catch it produces looks like a generous hitbox rather than like
9407               	; 	' arithmetic. The obstacle test has the same shape but cannot reach it --
9408               	; 	' obx tops out at 240 and klx at XWALL, so neither sum passes 255.
9409               	; 	IF klx > hx THEN hdd = klx - hx ELSE hdd = hx - klx

9443               	; 	' KELLY FLASHES WHILE HE IS KNOCKED ABOUT. Hitting an obstacle set a
9444               	; 	' 20-frame `knock` and took nine seconds off the clock, and NOTHING on
9445               	; 	' screen said so -- the time simply went. A flash is the arcade's own
9446               	; 	' idiom for "that hit you", costs one variable, and is readable even
9447               	; 	' when the collision happened off the edge of the player's attention.

9470               cv710
9471               	; 	' KELLY IS SPRITES 0, 1 AND 2 and nothing else ever is -- the VDP drops
9472               	; 	' the highest-numbered sprites on an over-full scanline, so the lowest
9473               	; 	' slots are the ones that can never disappear, and the player is the one
9474               	; 	' thing that must never disappear.
9475               	; 	'
9476               	; 	' THE BAND SPRITES SHARE A y ON PURPOSE. Slots 0 and 1 both sit at ky and
9477               	; 	' between them cover rows 0-15; slot 2 sits at ky+16. Boxes 0/1 and box 2
9478               	; 	' never share a scanline, so an actor costs at most TWO boxes on any line
9479               	; 	' -- and the VDP counts boxes, not pixels, so an empty overlap would have
9480               	; 	' cost just as much as a full one. Two actors meeting is four, exactly
9481               	; 	' the per-line limit, which is why obstacles are suppressed on Harry's
9482               	; 	' floor rather than merely being a kindness.
9483               	; 	ky = flry(klv)
9484 E170 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLV,r0
     E172 2099

     E17C 21BF

9607               	; 		' divide -- `/` compiles to a real TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and
9608               	; 		' this is per-frame code. Two adjacent bits give 0,4,8,12.

10336               	;
10337               	; 	' ======================================================================
10338               	; 	' THE SCANNER
10339               	; 	' ======================================================================
10340               	; 	' Rows 21-23, 16 chars wide, centred: 48 characters whose PATTERNS are
10341               	; 	' poked directly, so a moving dot costs two writes and no name-table
10342               	; 	' traffic. With the flip (DESIGN.md 2a) this is how you know where Harry
10343               	; 	' is at all -- he can be one pixel off-screen and completely invisible.
10344               	; 	'
10345               	; 	' Rows 21-23 all sit in the THIRD screen third, so every scanner pattern
10346               	; 	' is at base 4096 and no third-selection arithmetic is needed.
10347               	; 	'
10348               	; 	' BOTH DOTS ARE WHITE and Kelly's BLINKS. The manual wants Kelly black
10349               	; 	' and Harry white, but this is the TMS9918's two-colours-per-8x1-cell
10350               	; 	' mode: a black dot and a white dot on the same pixel row of the same
10351               	; 	' character cannot both exist. Blinking distinguishes them with one
10352               	; 	' colour, which is what RallyX does with its player dot.
10353               	; 	' THE STEPS MOVE, AND EVERY ESCALATOR ON SCREEN MOVES WITH THEM. A flight
10354               	; 	' is twelve characters -- six per direction, sliced out of a rendered
10355               	; 	' 64x40 bitmap by genart.py -- so rewriting those twelve PATTERNS animates
10356               	; 	' the lot: 96 bytes, no name-table traffic, and the same cost whether one
10357               	; 	' flight is visible or ten. The phases slide the cells ALONG the slope, so
10358               	; 	' the steps travel up the flight rather than drifting sideways.
10359               	; esc_tick:
10360               cvb_ESC_TICK
10361               	; 	' ONLY SCREENS 0 AND 7 CARRY A FLIGHT, so six screens in eight skip this
10362               	; 	' entirely -- which is what makes rewriting the cells every frame
10363               	; 	' affordable. Nested, not `klsc > 0 AND klsc < 7`: the 9900 backend
10364               	; 	' miscompiles a compare-AND-compare (CLAUDE.md 3A).
10365               	; 	IF klsc > 0 THEN
10366 E8CC D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     E8CE 217A     
10367 E8D0 0201  20 	li r1,256   ; 1*256
     E8D2 0100     
10368 E8D4 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
10369 E8D6 1402  14 	jhe cv782
10370 E8D8 0460  28 	b @cv781
     E8DA E8F0     
10371               cv782
10372               	; 		IF klsc < 7 THEN RETURN
10373 E8DC D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     E8DE 217A     
10374 E8E0 0201  20 	li r1,1792   ; 7*256
     E8E2 0700     
10375 E8E4 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
10376 E8E6 1A02  14 	jl cv784
10377 E8E8 0460  28 	b @cv783
     E8EA E8F0     
10378               cv784
10379 E8EC C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
10380 E8EE 0450  20 	b *r0
10381               cv783
10382               	; 	END IF
10383               cv781
10384               	; 	' ONE PHASE PER PASS, AND NEVER MORE. There are four phases covering an
10385               	; 	' 8 px period, so advancing by the frame delta ALIASES: at fdv 2 the
10386               	; 	' pattern flips between two positions half a period apart and the
10387               	; 	' direction stops being readable, and at fdv 3 the sequence runs 0,3,2,1
10388               	; 	' -- the staircase visibly runs BACKWARDS, carrying its steps down while
10389               	; 	' the rider goes up. That is the wagon-wheel effect, and no amount of
10390               	; 	' phase arithmetic fixes it: a 4-phase cycle can only be stepped by 1.
10391               	; 	'
10392               	; 	' So the ANIMATION is the clock here, and the rider is paced from it --
10393               	; 	' see move_kelly, which advances him by the same fixed 2-and-1 per pass
10394               	; 	' rather than by fdv. Locking the two together is the only way they
10395               	; 	' cannot drift, and it is what "the player moves at the rate the steps
10396               	; 	' carry him" actually means. The cost is that a ride takes 36 passes
10397               	; 	' rather than 36 frames, so it slows down when the loop does -- which is
10398               	; 	' the honest behaviour for a machine that is carrying you.
10399               	; 	escp = escp + 1
10400 E8F0 D020  34 	movb @cvb_ESCP,r0
     E8F2 20B0     
10401 E8F4 0220  22 	ai r0,256	; 1
     E8F6 0100

     E8FA 20B0

10412               	; 	' ONLY THE CELLS THAT MOVE, AND ONLY THIS SCREEN'S. genart.py measures
10413               	; 	' which cells move -- the step cells and the COMPOSITE copies of them
10414               	; 	' that cross a floor -- and groups them WEST then EAST, because only one
10415               	; 	' direction is ever on screen: screen 0 carries a west flight and screen
10416               	; 	' 7 an east one.
10417               	; 	'
10418               	; 	' THIS IS THE ONLY PER-PASS WORK UNIQUE TO THESE TWO SCREENS, and it is
10419               	; 	' `define_char`, which in bitmap mode is LDIRVM3 -- the TRIPLE copy, once
10420               	; 	' per screen third. It therefore costs three times what the character
10421               	; 	' count suggests, which is why these screens run slower than the rest.
10422               	; 	'
10423               	; 	' THE WEST SCREEN USED TO COST HALF AS MUCH AGAIN, because it carries TWO
10424               	; 	' flights: floor 1's crosses into a shop floor and floor 3's into the
10425               	; 	' roof, so it needed both composite sets -- nine characters against the
10426               	; 	' east's six, 216 bytes a pass against 144. It measured 20 passes a
10427               	; 	' second where the east screen managed 24-26. The two sets are
10428               	; 	' PIXEL-IDENTICAL and are never wanted in the same screen THIRD, so they
10429               	; 	' share their characters now and are told apart by the per-third colour
10430               	; 	' table (see esc_deck_col in setup). Six either side.
10431               	; 	'
10432               	; 	' The handrail and the frame sit past the end of both ranges and never
10433               	; 	' move at all.

10693 EB4A C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
10694 EB4C 0450  20 	b *r0
10695               	;
10696               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------- the scanner's furniture
10697               	; 	' Without this the scanner is two dots in a void: you can see THAT Harry is
10698               	; 	' somewhere, but not what he is near, which is the one thing you actually
10699               	; 	' need when he is off-screen. Floor lines say which level, the slashes say
10700               	; 	' where that floor's escalator is, and the bar says where the elevator is --
10701               	; 	' exactly the three things the manual lists.
10702               	; 	'
10703               	; 	' FURNITURE AND DOTS ARE SEGREGATED BY PIXEL ROW ON PURPOSE, and a level
10704               	; 	' band is now FOUR rows, one per colour, because a pixel row carries
10705               	; 	' exactly one colour here:
10706               	; 	'
10707               	; 	'   +0  GREY    escalator head, and the elevator car
10708               	; 	'   +1  GREY    the air of that floor
10709               	; 	'   +2  GREY
10710               	; 	'   +3  YELLOW  the floor line -- and NOTHING else goes here
10711               	; 	'
10712               	; 	' THE FIRST THREE ROWS ARE THE FLOOR'S AIR AND EVERYTHING LIVES IN THEM:
10713               	; 	' the escalator's three-step diagonal, the lift car, and both actors, all
10714               	; 	' three pixels tall. They are grey in the table, and the Kop and the
10715               	; 	' crook recolour their own CHARACTER as they move (scan_mark) -- which is
10716               	; 	' what lets both of them fill the band instead of getting a pixel row
10717               	; 	' each. Eight pixels of this canvas is half a screen of the store, so
10718               	; 	' they are almost never in one character; when they are, they merge.
10719               	; 	'
10720               	; 	' Shrinking from six rows bought eight empty pixel rows, four above the
10721               	; 	' instrument and four below, so it no longer butts against the shop floor
10722               	; 	' above it or the bottom of the screen.
10723               	; 	'
10724               	; 	' The dots are erased by ANDing their bits out, so anything sharing a row
10725               	; 	' with one is rubbed away wherever an actor has passed -- which is why
10726               	; 	' scan_escs and scan_elev redraw every tick rather than once.
10727               	; scan_furn:
10728               cvb_SCAN_FURN

11146               	;
11147               	; 	' Blank the canvas by WRITING zeros rather than copying 384 of them out

11150               	; 	' back the best part of half a kilobyte, which is the budget that
11151               	; 	' actually binds. Paced in bursts: a few hundred VDP writes in a single
11152               	; 	' frame are silently dropped (CLAUDE.md 3A).

11206               	; 	' One actor per tick, at about 10 Hz: erase where it was, draw where it

11226               	;
11227               	; 	' THE TWO ACTORS ARE SPRITES; EVERYTHING ELSE IS CHARACTERS.
11228               	; 	'
11229               	; 	' That split is what makes the instrument simple. The floor lines, the
11230               	; 	' flights and the lift car do not move relative to the canvas, so they
11231               	; 	' are pixels in the scanner's own characters and cost nothing to keep.
11232               	; 	' The Kop and the crook DO move, and as sprites they need no colour
11233               	; 	' table, no erase and no priority arithmetic -- they draw over the
11234               	; 	' furniture because sprites always do, and nothing shifts to make room.
11235               	; 	'
11236               	; 	' IT ALSO REMOVED THE BLINK. Kelly flashed because a pixel row of this
11237               	; 	' canvas can only carry one colour, so at close range the two markers
11238               	; 	' merged and the blink was the only thing left to tell them apart. Two
11239               	; 	' sprites are two colours wherever they stand.
11240               	; 	'
11241               	; 	' The furniture is drawn ONCE, by scan_furn. It used to be redrawn every
11242               	; 	' tick because erasing a character-based marker ANDed bits out of
11243               	; 	' whatever it was standing on; there is nothing left to rub it away. The
11244               	; 	' car still redraws itself, because the car actually moves.
11245               	; 	' -- the lift car. IT WAS FLICKERING, and the cause was not the drawing
11246               	; 	' but the ERASING: scan_elev cleared its three cells and put them back
11247               	; 	' every tick, whether or not the car had moved, so several times a second
11248               	; 	' there was a window with the pixels off. A sprite has no erase at all --
11249               	; 	' it is placed, and the VDP does the rest -- and slot 26 sits BELOW the
11250               	; 	' two markers, so the Kop and the crook pass over it without either of
11251               	; 	' them having to know the car exists.
11252               	; 	say = 3 - elvl
11253 EF38 D020  34 	movb @cvb_ELVL,r0
     EF3A 2131     
11254 EF3C 0980  32 	srl r0,8
11255 EF3E C040  18 	mov r0,r1
11256 EF40 0200  20 	li r0,3
     EF42 0003

11917               	; 	' THE CAST STAYS ON SCREEN FOR THE COUNT. Hiding everything first threw

11954 F504 B708     	data cvb_START_KROOK
11955               	; 	GOTO main
11956 F506 0460  28 	b @cvb_MAIN
     F508 AA16     
11957               	;
11958               	; 	' THE BONUS IS COUNTED OUT, NOT AWARDED. Dropping the whole sum into the
11959               	; 	' score in one frame tells the player a number; ticking the clock down a
11960               	; 	' unit at a time, with the score climbing and a beat under each step,
11961               	; 	' tells them what the number was FOR. It is the pay-off for every second
11962               	; 	' they saved, and every arcade game of this era spends a moment on it.
11963               	; 	'
11964               	; 	' It also costs nothing to get right: the loop already has to run down
11965               	; 	' `tsec`, so the animation IS the calculation, and the repeated-addition
11966               	; 	' note below stops applying -- each step adds one band's worth.
11967               	; 	'
11968               	; 	' x100 for Krooks 1-9, x200 for 10-15, x300 from 16. Two independent
11969               	; 	' sources agree on those bands; the manual's own wording suggests
11970               	; 	' 1-8 / 9-16 / 17+ and DESIGN.md 0 records the disagreement.
11971               	; 	'
11972               	; 	' #bval MUST BE 16-BIT: 300 does not fit in a byte, and a plain variable
11973               	; 	' would silently truncate it to 44 (CLAUDE.md 3A).
11974               	; bonus_count:
11975               cvb_BONUS_COUNT
11976               	; 	#bval = 100
11977 F50A 0200  20 	li r0,100
     F50C 0064

     F5F0 F542     
12077               	;
12078               	; 	' `calc_bonus` USED TO LIVE HERE and computed the whole sum by repeated
12079               	; 	' addition, to dodge two hazards at once: `tsec * bmul` is an 8-bit
12080               	; 	' product (a 50-unit capture in the x300 band pays 15,000, six times past
12081               	; 	' a byte), and multiplying properly lands on the MPY/r0 hazard where a
12082               	; 	' 16-bit variable read straight after being multiplied returns the
12083               	; 	' product's high word. Counting the bonus out a unit at a time (above)
12084               	; 	' removes the multiply entirely rather than working around it -- the loop
12085               	; 	' the player watches IS the arithmetic.
12086               	; 	'
12087               	; 	' It also fixed the last-tick case for free: `bn_loop` tests `tsec = 0`
12088               	; 	' on entry, where the old `FOR bi = 1 TO tsec` would have run its body
12089               	; 	' once and paid a full multiplier for no time at all (CLAUDE.md 3A).
12090               	;

     F720 AA16     
12225               	;
12226               	; 	' Silence every channel FIRST. SOUND latches, and the per-channel decay
12227               	; 	' counters are not ticked inside this loop -- a tone still sounding when
12228               	; 	' the pause starts would hang for its whole duration, which is the classic
12229               	; 	' CVBasic sticky-audio failure.
12230               	; 	' EVERY CHANNEL OFF, AND THE DECAY COUNTERS WITH THEM. The counters are
12231               	; 	' ticked by the main loop, so anything still sounding when the loop stops
12232               	; 	' -- for a capture, a death, the bonus count -- sustains for as long as
12233               	; 	' whatever replaced the loop takes. That is where the long beep over the
12234               	; 	' bonus tally came from: not the tally's own note, which turns itself off
12235               	; 	' after every unit, but a footstep or a hit that was still ringing when
12236               	; 	' Harry was caught.
12237               	; snd_off:
12238               cvb_SND_OFF

12336               	; 	' ======================================================================
12337               	; 	' SOUND
12338               	; 	' ======================================================================
12339               	; 	' Every effect gets an explicit note-off. SOUND latches -- with no
12340               	; 	' `SOUND ch,f,0` the last tone sustains for ever. And the second argument
12341               	; 	' is a 10-BIT DIVISOR, max 1023: SMALLER IS HIGHER, so a rising sweep is
12342               	; 	' written as a falling number and anything over 1023 is silently masked
12343               	; 	' to an unrelated pitch.
12344               	; sfx_tick:
12345               cvb_SFX_TICK

12690 FAF4 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0

12692               	;
12693               	; 	' The font stays in bank 0: it is small, and keeping one readable thing
12694               	; 	' unbanked makes a bank-selection mistake obvious (text survives, art does
12695               	; 	' not) instead of producing a uniformly blank screen.
12696               	; 	INCLUDE "font.bas"
12697               	; 	' Keystone Kapers font -- GENERATED by assets/genfont.py. Do not edit; regenerate.
12698               	; 	'
12699               	; 	' Characters 32-90 (59 of them), 8 bytes each = 472 bytes. Williams' cabinet face:
12700               	; 	' heavy, squared, flat terminals, no serifs -- drawn 5x7 inside the 8x8 cell so a
12701               	; 	' blank column at the right and a blank row at the bottom keep it legible.
12702               	; 	'
12703               	; 	' Loaded with DEFINE CHAR 32,59,font_bits. The run must be CONTIGUOUS, so slots
12704               	; 	' with no glyph emit blanks rather than being skipped -- a gap would shift every
12705               	; 	' letter after it.
12706               	;
12707               	; font_bits:
12708               cvb_FONT_BITS
12709               	; 	DATA BYTE $00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00	' space
12710 FAF8 0000     	byte >00,>00,>00,>00,>00,>00,>00,>00
     FAFA 0000     
     FAFC 0000     
     FAFE 0000     
12711               	; 	DATA BYTE $20,$20,$20,$20,$20,$00,$20,$00	' !
12712 FB00 2020     	byte >20,>20,>20,>20,>20,>00,>20,>00
     FB02 2020     
     FB04 2000     
     FB06 2000     
12713               	; 	DATA BYTE $50,$50,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00	' "
12714 FB08 5050     	byte >50,>50,>00,>00,>00,>00,>00,>00
     FB0A 0000     
     FB0C 0000     
     FB0E 0000     
12715               	; 	DATA BYTE $50,$F8,$50,$50,$F8,$50,$00,$00	' #
12716 FB10 50F8     	byte >50,>f8,>50,>50,>f8,>50,>00,>00
     FB12 5050     
     FB14 F850     
     FB16 0000

12827               	;
12828               	; 	' EVERYTHING BELOW THIS LINE IS ASSEMBLED INTO BANK 1, so the INCLUDE order
12829               	; 	' is load-bearing and nothing but data may follow it. Putting the directive
12830               	; 	' above font.bas would sweep the font in too.
12831               	; 	#if TI994A
12832               	; 	BANK 1
12833      032E     BANK_0_FREE:	EQU >fffe-$

12840               	; 	INCLUDE "art.bas"
12841               	; 	' Keystone Kapers art -- GENERATED by assets/genart.py. Do not edit; regenerate.
12842               	; 	'
12843               	; 	' EVERY SPRITE IS 16x16: sprite size and magnification are GLOBAL on the
12844               	; 	' TMS9918, so small objects are drawn small INSIDE a 16x16 box rather than
12845               	; 	' being small sprites.
12846               	; 	'
12847               	; 	' EVERY 8-PX OBJECT SITS IN ROWS 8-15 OF ITS BOX, ducked Kelly included, so
12848               	; 	' one rule places them all: bottom edge = y + 16, and a thing resting on the
12849               	; 	' floor is at y = FLOORY - 16.
12850               	; 	'
12851               	; 	' Sprite bytes are QUADRANT-ORDERED: 16 rows of the left half, then 16 of
12852               	; 	' the right. Every block is an EVEN number of bytes -- an odd DATA BYTE run
12853               	; 	' silently misaligns every word table after it (CLAUDE.md 3A).
12854               	;
12855               	; spr_kelly:	' Kelly: RIGHT hat/face/tunic/tunic-arm-up/duck-hat/duck-face, then the same LEFT (+24), then the FOUR shared run frames. Patterns 0..60
12856               cvb_SPR_KELLY

13637               	; 	' -------------------------------------------------- scanner canvas
13638               	; 	' Chars 208-255: 16 cols x 3 rows over screen rows 21-23. Blank to start;
13639               	; 	' dots are plotted by VPOKEing the PATTERN table (rows 21-23 sit in the
13640               	; 	' third screen third, so every scanner pattern is at base 4096).
13641               	;
13642               	; 	' --------------------------------------------------- font colours
13643               	; 	' Chars 32-90, EIGHT bytes each. Without this the font keeps whatever
13644               	; 	' CVBasic left in the colour table, which on a green store made the HUD
13645               	; 	' unreadable.
13646               	;
13647               	; scan_col3:	' one 8-byte block per canvas row: 4 px of GREY margin, then a 3 px band of furniture GREY and a yellow floor line, per 4 px level, on dark green. The Kop and the crook are coloured PER CHARACTER at run time -- see scan_set

13749               	; 	INCLUDE "store.bas"
13750               	; 	' Keystone Kapers store -- GENERATED by assets/genstore.py. Do not edit.
13751               	; 	'
13752               	; 	' EIGHT band templates of 5 rows x 32 cols (160 bytes each) plus a 32-byte
13753               	; 	' index over (level, screen). The whole 8-screen, 4-level building is about
13754               	; 	' 1.3 KB instead of the 5,120 bytes it would cost to store literally.
13755               	; 	'
13756               	; 	' All eight templates are ONE contiguous block under ONE label, so a band
13757               	; 	' blit is
13758               	; 	'     SCREEN stor_tpl, tpl*160, toprow*32, 32, 5, 32
13759               	; 	' with no label ladder -- SCREEN needs a literal label and a label cannot be
13760               	; 	' chosen by a variable, which is why RallyX needed a 4-way IF over its maze
13761               	; 	' tables. One block sidesteps it.
13762               	; 	'
13763               	; 	' 160 is EVEN, so this cannot leave the location counter odd and misalign
13764               	; 	' the word tables defined after it.
13765               	; 	'
13766               	; 	' template ids:
13767               	; 	'   0 T_AISLE_A
13768               	; 	'   1 T_AISLE_B
13769               	; 	'   2 T_ESC_W
13770               	; 	'   3 T_ESC_E
13771               	; 	'   4 T_ELEV
13772               	; 	'   5 T_ROOF
13773               	; 	'   6 T_ROOF_W
13774               	; 	'   7 T_ROOF_E
13775               	; 	'   8 T_END_W
13776               	; 	'   9 T_END_E
13777               	;
13778               	; stor_tpl:	' 10 templates x 160 bytes
13779               cvb_STOR_TPL
13780               	; 	DATA BYTE 151,151,151,151,107,151,151,151,151,151,151,107,151,151,151,151
